Mandate backend Phase 3 verification record (REST identity / agent vertical slice)

Changed branch/field
- Backend phase: shared REST API entry point and authenticated agent lifecycle, matching architecture sections 3, 8, and 9.
- MODIFIED_FILE: /Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/apps/api/src/app.ts
- Implemented `GET /health/live`, `GET /health/ready`, public `GET /api/v1/openapi.json`, authenticated `GET /api/v1/me`, tenant-scoped `GET /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/agents`, and idempotent `POST /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/agents`.
- Human identity requires RS256 JWT validation against configured OIDC issuer, audience, HTTPS JWKS endpoint (loopback HTTP only for local development), and `exp`/`sub` claims. Membership is loaded from Postgres; cross-tenant lookups return 404 and only OWNER/ADMIN can register agents. Agent credentials are 256-bit random bearer tokens, stored as SHA-256 digests and rejected after revocation/expiry/key-version mismatch.
- Agent registration runs in a Postgres transaction, locks/rechecks the membership and idempotency scope, and writes the agent, credential digest, idempotency response, audit row, and outbox event atomically. A replay omits the one-time token; same idempotency key with a different request returns 409.
- Added strict request/error schemas, a Zod-derived OpenAPI 3.1 document, API process entry point, and migrations for agent credentials/idempotency.
- MODIFIED_FILE SHA-256: `a85ab24396bf9830ecf2948e4c53724018989cab1d783d250eac0452b0165a6e`

Security review
- Security-sensitive: YES; OWASP review covered authentication, tenant/role checks, SQL parameterization, credential handling, request validation/body limits, error redaction, logging, dependency audit, and audit/outbox atomicity.

Agent secrets are generated randomly, never persisted raw, and are returned only in the create response; idempotent replay does not disclose them.

Mandate backend Phase 6 verification record (outbox worker and alert feed)

Changed branch/field
- Backend phase: a separate PostgreSQL outbox consumer materializes tenant-scoped in-product alerts, matching architecture sections 3–4 and runtime component 2.
- MODIFIED_FILE: /Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/apps/worker/src/outbox-worker.ts
- Added migration `005_alert_notifications.sql`, `apps/worker` polling process and scripts, `GET /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/alerts`, and typed alert projection/read APIs.
- Worker claims bounded batches using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; row leases are reclaimable after 60 seconds. Claim increments attempts. Alert insert and outbox delivered mark share one transaction and use a unique outbox-event key. Failures release the lease with capped exponential retry scheduling and sanitized error code. Projection allowlists event kinds/titles; unknown payloads are dropped and unknown aggregate IDs become the outbox UUID.
- Real PostgreSQL tests cover concurrent workers, target-event single projection, duplicate processing, fallback category, and no secret-value projection. A live worker process also drained pending events and shut down on SIGINT.

Mandate backend Phase 8 verification record (Safe guard + agent module execution boundary)

Changed branch/field
- Added actual Safe execution-boundary enforcement for a bounded action set, aligned to architecture sections 2–3, 6–9, and Phase C. `MandateSafeGuard` implements both Safe transaction guard and module guard, and `MandateAgentModule` validates EIP-712 agent signatures before routing the exact call through the Safe.
- MODIFIED_FILE: /Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/contracts/src/MandateSafeGuard.sol
- The guard allows only direct native-currency transfers and canonical ERC-20 `transfer(address,uint256)` calls; all delegatecall/other selectors fail closed. It binds per-Safe policy hash/monotonic epoch/expiry, approved assets/recipients, per-action and per-asset window caps, and max action count. Safe transactions require zero gas reimbursement fields; failed module-path execution reverts the entire outer call, rolling back guard counters and agent nonce.
- Agent signature fields bind the Safe, chain ID, module, policy epoch, agent EOA, monotonic key version, exact target/value/calldata hash, deadline, and agent nonce. ECDSA rejects non-65-byte, high-s, invalid-v and zero-recovery signatures.
- The real Safe test deploys the pinned Safe 1.5.0 singleton plus proxy on local Anvil 1.7.1; contract source compiles with pinned Solidity 0.8.36, optimizer (200 runs), and `viaIR`. Both transaction and module guard slots are installed and `isFullyInstalled()` is asserted before successful actions.
- Explicitly incomplete: Mandate API action service is not wired to submit these module calls; policy activation/revocation API and grant/account registry are not integrated with the contract; no Monad testnet deployment, Safe setup transaction builder, indexer, reorg handling, chain receipt adapter, invariant/fuzz campaign, formal verification, or independent contract audit exists. Owner-threshold Safe recovery can intentionally remove either guard; the missing indexer/API must mark that account unsupported immediately. No claim of production readiness.

Mandate backend Phase 9 verification record (on-chain exact-call approval thresholds)

Changed branch/field
- Added on-chain owner approval enforcement to policy execution for actions above an asset-specific threshold. `configurePolicy` now uses `PolicyConfig` to keep the ABI/code-generation stack bounded; `MandateAgentModule` consumes the approval before executing through the Safe. The approval binds Safe, chain, revision and epoch, agent address, key version, nonce, target, value, calldata hash, and action deadline. Successful action consumes it once. A changed call does not consume it; any later Safe failure reverts consumed approval, module nonce, and spend counters.

Mandate backend Phase 18 verification record (exact-action authorization primitive)

Changed branch/field
- Added `packages/chain/src/action-execution-authorization.ts`, a pure, typed planner for the architecture's supported native/ERC-20 transfer subset. Mandate backend Phase 22 verification record (durable execution submission API)

Changed branch/field
- Added `POST /api/v1/orgs/:orgId/actions/:actionId/execute` for authenticated agent principals. The service reloads the persisted authorization, verifies the agent's EIP-712 signature and the exact caller-signed outer transaction, then submits only those signed bytes through a configured RPC adapter.

Mandate backend Phase 23 verification record (canonical execution receipt reconciliation)

Changed branch/field
- Added an execution receipt polling service to the existing worker. It selects only `SUBMITTED` attempts with stored transaction hashes and reads receipts through RPC URLs held in server configuration; no RPC URL or transaction bytes are user controlled or persisted.
- `EvmExecutionReceiptReader` verifies chain ID, transaction hash, non-null receipt status, canonical block hash twice around the finality read, and an explicitly configured confirmation count. It emits metadata-only `TENTATIVE` or `FINAL` observations for successful or reverted transactions; pending receipts produce no settlement.
- `ExecutionReconciliationStore` serializes per-action observations and atomically records/upserts the shared receipt projection. A tentative receipt that disappears before finality becomes `REORGED` and the action remains pending/submitted; re-inclusion updates the evidence. A final successful receipt moves attempt `SUBMITTED -> CONFIRMED`, action `SUBMITTED -> CONFIRMED -> RECONCILED`, and reservation `ACTIVE -> CONSUMED`. A final status-0 receipt moves the attempt through `REVERTED`, the action through `REVERTED -> RECONCILED`, and reservation `ACTIVE -> RELEASED`. Receipt state, transitions, hash-linked audit events, and outbox events commit together. Replays after settlement are idempotent.
- Added migration `014_execution_reconciliation.sql` with polling/activity indexes, worker configuration validation for matching chain/RPC and confirmation maps, and alert projections for tentative/reorged/reconciled/reverted events. API receipt list reads the same durable projection.

Mandate backend Phase 24 verification record (canonical fee-replacement reconciliation)

Changed branch/field
- When the originally submitted hash has no receipt, the reconciler checks finalized `indexed_chain_logs` for the exact `AgentActionExecuted(address,uint64,uint256,bytes32)` event emitted by the authorization's module and matching agent address, key version, module execution nonce, and typed action hash. A match selects the actual mined transaction hash; the RPC receipt reader then verifies that hash and its canonical block/finality.
- A replacement receipt is accepted only if the exact indexed execution event still matches and the receipt indicates successful execution. Finalization writes the mined replacement hash to `execution_attempts.transaction_hash`, leaves the originally submitted hash/idempotency record intact, settles the reservation once, and writes an `ACTION_TRANSACTION_REPLACED` hash-linked audit/outbox event plus the normal final reconciliation event. Raw transaction bytes remain unpersisted.
- Added a transaction-replaced alert projection and unit/PostgreSQL integration tests. The database fixture proves a submitted hash becomes tentatively observed, disappears/reorgs, and is then reconciled to a distinct transaction only when its canonical module event matches all typed authorization fields.

Mandate backend Phase 25 verification record (proof-bound dropped transaction settlement)

Changed branch/field
- New execution submissions persist the caller-signed outer transaction sender and nonce, in addition to the transaction hash; raw signed bytes remain unpersisted.
- A missing original receipt first checks exact finalized `AgentActionExecuted` evidence for a fee replacement. Only when that is absent may the reconciler consider a drop. It obtains the persisted indexer's finalized checkpoint only when the known `indexed_from_block` begins at/before the pinned authorization snapshot and finality has reached the snapshot; the chain reader verifies chain ID and checkpoint block hash before and after reading sender nonce/timestamp. Drop eligibility requires that finalized sender nonce exceed the signed nonce and that the checkpoint timestamp be strictly later than the stored authorization deadline.
- `markDropped` locks and rechecks the action, attempt, authorization, submission, reservation, account module, indexer cursor and checkpoint block in a single PostgreSQL transaction; it checks that no exact matching execution event exists through the checkpoint. It atomically transitions attempt `SUBMITTED -> DROPPED`, action `SUBMITTED -> DROPPED -> RECONCILED`, reservation `ACTIVE -> RELEASED`, and appends one hash-linked `ACTION_DROPPED` audit event/outbox event. Replays or missing evidence do not settle twice. Historic submissions and indexer cursors without recorded proof boundaries remain pending.
- Added `015_execution_drop_evidence.sql`, indexer coverage-boundary persistence, dropped alert projection, unit tests for positive/expiry conditions, real PostgreSQL coverage for unknown historical range and exact-event negative guards, indexer boundary persistence checks, and Anvil sender-nonce/checkpoint reader assertions.

Mandate backend Phase 26 verification record (deep post-finality reorg compensation)

Changed branch/field
- The worker periodically rechecks each settled `FINAL` receipt against the live RPC's canonical block at the receipt height. The reader verifies the configured chain ID, reads the canonical block twice around the head/checkpoint read, and returns a confirmation count. DB candidates are limited to receipts covered by the persisted indexer start boundary and finalized indexer height; a changed live block hash is acted upon only after the replacement block also meets configured finality.
- `markDeepReorg` rechecks and locks action, attempt, receipt, reservation, account, and canonical indexer rows in one PostgreSQL transaction. On an independently indexed, finalized replacement block it changes receipt/attempt/action to `REORGED`, reactivates the reservation as a budget hold, pauses the affected ACTIVE account, and records `ACTION_DEEP_REORG_DETECTED` plus account-protection audit/outbox evidence. Replays are idempotent. Reorged actions are held for operator resolution and are never auto-resubmitted.
- Added `016_deep_reorg_compensation.sql`, explicit `RECONCILED -> REORGED` domain state, periodic receipt-check timestamps/index, service counters/backoff, alert projections, Anvil canonical-block checks, service finality tests, and a real PostgreSQL state/incident test.
- MODIFIED_FILE: `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/packages/adapters/src/postgres/execution-reconciliation-store.ts`

Mandate backend Phase 27 verification checkpoint — signed webhook delivery plumbing

Changed branch/field
- Added migration `017_signed_webhooks.sql`: nullable secret reference and endpoint lifecycle timestamps, scoped endpoint/outbox uniqueness, and durable per-endpoint delivery records with bounded status, attempts, retry timing, coarse error codes, tenant-composite foreign keys, and polling/history indexes.
- The outbox projector inserts matching enabled endpoint deliveries in the same PostgreSQL transaction as alert projection and event completion. `WebhookDeliveryWorker` claims with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, constructs a stable delivery envelope, signs `${timestamp}.${body}` using HMAC-SHA256, records 2xx success, and uses capped exponential backoff with a terminal limit of 12 attempts. It filters sensitive field names recursively and bounds nesting/arrays/string sizes.
- `PublicHttpsWebhookTransport` enforces HTTPS/443, rejects credentials/fragments/local/reserved/private addresses and any DNS result that is not public, re-resolves at connection time to constrain DNS rebinding, does not follow redirects, bounds request body/header size, and times out requests. A code review found Node's shared `BlockList` gave incorrect IPv4 results after IPv6 ranges were inserted; separate IPv4/IPv6 blocklists fixed it, and IPv6 additionally requires membership in global-unicast `2000::/3`; tests cover public/private mixed answers, mapped addresses, and out-of-range IPv6.
- The worker supports an optional organization filter for deterministic isolated integration tests; production invocation remains unfiltered.

Mandate backend Phase 28 verification checkpoint — authenticated webhook endpoint management

Changed branch/field
- Added tenant-admin webhook lifecycle operations over `/api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/webhooks`: list/create, enable/disable, rotate secret, soft-delete, and read delivery history. OWNER/ADMIN membership is checked at both API boundary and write transaction. Delivery-history SQL now repeats role authorization in the data query to avoid a membership-revocation race between access check and record read.
- Endpoint creation validates public HTTPS URLs before provisioning; it issues a cryptographically random 256-bit base64url signing secret once, stores only a secret-manager reference, and does not re-display a secret on idempotent replay. Rotation tracks a prior secret reference until cleanup succeeds; deletion disables and soft-deletes before secret deletion and retains delivery history. Mutations persist idempotency outcomes, hash-linked audit events, and outbox events transactionally.

Mandate backend Phase 29 verification checkpoint — organization member and role management

Changed branch/field
- Added authenticated tenant team routes: `GET /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/members`, `PUT /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/members/{subject}` (add or change role), and `DELETE /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/members/{subject}`. Membership identity binds to the authenticated OIDC subject; OWNER/ADMIN manage members, but only OWNER can grant, change, or remove OWNER role.
- The PostgreSQL store serializes membership mutations by locking the organization row. A transaction rechecks the actor's current role, enforces that at least one OWNER remains, then commits the membership update/removal, scoped idempotency outcome, hash-linked audit event, and outbox event atomically. Reads authorize membership in the same SQL statement. Concurrent owner-removal integration test proves one competing removal receives 204, the revoked caller receives 404, and one OWNER remains.
- Added strict OpenAPI schemas/routes and real PostgreSQL integration tests for list/add/replay/conflict, admin/viewer denials, owner grant limits, owner transfer, final-owner hold, membership removal/audit events, and concurrent owner removals. Existing provider, policy, and webhook behavior remains covered.

Mandate backend Phase 30 verification checkpoint — local MCP stdio interface

Changed branch/field
- Added `apps/mcp-server` as a separate local MCP stdio process backed by the authenticated REST API and official TypeScript MCP SDK. Six typed tools provide policy list/get, deterministic action simulation, agent action request, action detail, and receipt lookup. Organization context is established at startup from `/api/v1/me`; agent organization is fixed to its authenticated identity, while a human with multiple memberships must configure a matching `MANDATE_ORGANIZATION_ID`. No arbitrary execution, activation, approval, or revocation tools are exposed. The process sends bearer credentials only to a validated HTTPS REST endpoint (loopback HTTP for local development), applies an 8-second timeout, and keeps protocol output on stdout while process errors go to stderr.
- Added in-process protocol/client tests and a subprocess stdio integration test against an HTTP API fixture; updated commands, environment sample, and setup documentation. The local stdio process does not require a model-provider API key. Hosted MCP OAuth remains separate.

Mandate backend Phase 32 verification checkpoint — organization invitations

Changed branch/field
- Added `POST /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/invitations` for OWNER/ADMIN invite creation; only OWNER can invite an OWNER. Creation is transactional and idempotent. It returns a random 256-bit URL-safe token once, stores only a SHA-256 token hash, records a seven-day expiry, and omits the raw token from replay responses, PostgreSQL response JSON, audit, and outbox. Audit and outbox events identify the invitation by UUID rather than embedding its email/token.
- Added `POST /api/v1/invitations/accept`. Acceptance requires a valid human OIDC JWT whose signed claims contain `email_verified: true`; its normalized email must equal the invited email. The database transaction locks and validates the invitation, inserts the membership, marks the invitation accepted, and writes linked audit/outbox events. Matching expired tokens transition to `EXPIRED` with one audit/outbox event; token-free invitation summaries and replay JSON omit both the token and email. Same-subject retry replays the accepted state; another or unverified identity cannot claim it. `/api/v1/me` explicitly projects safe principal fields and never returns verified email.
- Added migration `020_organization_invitations.sql`, OpenAPI contracts, and real PostgreSQL tests covering token secrecy, replay, unverified/mismatched identity denial, owner-role gate, successful acceptance, and same-user retry. Token delivery is currently out-of-band by the organization administrator; automated email delivery and cancellation/list operations remain deployment/product work.
- MODIFIED_FILE: `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/packages/adapters/src/postgres/organization-invitation-store.ts`
- Other changed files: `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/migrations/020_organization_invitations.sql`; `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/packages/domain/src/principal.ts`; `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/apps/api/src/app.ts`; `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/apps/api/src/openapi.ts`; `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/apps/api/test/api.integration.test.ts`; `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/README.md`; `/Users/chipichipi/Documents/ChatGPT/Mandate/backend/VERIFICATION.txt`.

No new dependency was added; `npm audit` reports zero vulnerabilities.

A05/A01 configuration/CORS: HTTPS required outside loopback, Host validation, exact Origin allowlist, explicit proxy/TLS deployment requirements. A06: no dependencies added; audit clean. A09: errors are coarse and credentials are not logged. A04 XML and A10 SSRF are not applicable to this handler; the only outbound target is the deployment-configured Mandate API URL. Review status: PASS for Phase 40. Production still depends on configuring the OIDC provider to issue the MCP resource audience and on a trusted private TLS proxy.

## Phase 41 — Deep-reorg operator resolution and SDK/API integration

Changed branch/fields: added append-only `execution_reorg_resolutions` with a single immutable resolution per action; added `ExecutionReorgResolutionService` / `ExecutionReorgResolutionStore`; added `POST /api/v1/orgs/{orgId}/actions/{actionId}/reorg-resolution` and OpenAPI schema; added `MandateClient.resolveDeepReorg`. The OWNER-only transaction rechecks membership and incident state, persists `CONSUMED`/`RELEASED`, a bounded reason, optional evidence hash, and original/canonical block evidence; serializes by action and idempotency key; updates only the reservation; and appends hash-linked audit/outbox records and an indefinite idempotency result. Action/attempt/receipt remain `REORGED`; account remains `PAUSED`; no transaction is retried. Updated README and architecture route/recovery requirements. ## Phase 44 — encrypted invitation email delivery and checkpoint-order regression

Changed branch/fields: added an optional PostgreSQL-backed invitation-email delivery queue; AES-256-GCM encrypted one-time token ciphertext bound to organization and invitation IDs; SES v2 plain-text transport; bounded retry/backoff, cancellation on acceptance/revocation/expiry, terminal ciphertext erasure, worker configuration, API queue signal, migration 024, and backend documentation. Acceptance links place the token in the URL fragment. Also fixed Phase 43 audit checkpoint ordering: the text aliases `sequence` / `checkpoint_sequence` had shadowed numeric columns in `ORDER BY`, so event sequence 100 could sort before 99 and prevent new anchors. The store now sorts qualified numeric columns. No frontend was built.

## Phase 48 — deterministic randomized Safe execution invariants

Changed branch/fields: added a seeded 40-case randomized rejection/invariant property test to `packages/chain/test/safe-enforcement.integration.test.ts`. Four deterministic branches exercise over-limit ERC-20 amount, disallowed recipient, unsupported `approve` selector, and nonzero ETH value accompanying an ERC-20 transfer. For every rejected Safe execution the test compares on-chain action-window usage, policy epoch, agent/module nonce, Safe nonce, token spend, and recipient token balance to the pre-call snapshot; it asserts that every branch was actually generated. The 30-second test timeout accommodates 40 real Safe transactions. Contract README and architecture status clarify this is a bounded deterministic probe, not a general-purpose stateful fuzzer, formal verification, testnet deployment, or independent audit. Backend README keeps the outstanding architecture gates explicit. No production Solidity or frontend changed.

## Phase 49 — stateful Safe invariants and isolated Anvil test cases

Changed branch/fields: each Safe integration test now takes an Anvil `evm_snapshot` before execution and reverts it afterward, with the revert result asserted. Added a 40-step fixed-seed mixed sequence of agent-signed token calls: allowed transfers, above-per-action amounts, unauthorized recipients, and unsupported selectors. The property records on-chain state before each attempt, confirms exact positive deltas for accepted calls, confirms all tracked contract/asset state is unchanged after rejected calls, and proves the action-count cap admits exactly five valid calls and rejects subsequent valid attempts. Corrected the prior suite-order-dependent delegatecall/module test to explicitly execute five allowed transfers before checking the cap. Test and docs only; no Solidity/runtime code and no frontend changed.

## Phase 50 — chain-ID-validated indexer RPC read failover

Changed branch/fields: added `FailoverEvmIndexerRpc`, a sequential, sticky endpoint adapter for finalized indexer reads. Before an endpoint is used, it must return the configured chain ID; successfully validated endpoint identity is cached to avoid an extra identity RPC per block/log request. On endpoint failure, the adapter tries the remaining configured endpoints and emits only a generic exhaustion error, not provider URL/body details. The existing indexer still rejects discontinuous block ranges and logs whose block hashes disagree with scanned canonical blocks before committing. Added `MANDATE_INDEXER_RPC_FALLBACK_URLS` as optional chain-ID → ordered URL-array configuration; parser rejects unknown chains, empty/duplicate lists, primary duplicates, non-HTTPS remote URLs, credentials, and fragments. Compose exposes the indexer-only setting. Fallbacks do not change transaction broadcasting. Docs state that configured RPC providers remain trusted sources and that matching chain ID alone is not proof of canonical history.

## Phase 51 — chain-verified receipt reconciliation RPC read failover

Changed branch/fields: receipt reconciliation now builds a bounded sequential AbstractProvider from the configured primary plus up to three optional per-chain fallback URLs. Every endpoint is validated for HTTPS (or loopback HTTP for local testing), duplicate endpoints are rejected, redirects are disabled, probes have a three-second timeout, and every candidate must report the expected chain ID before it receives reads. Errors from one endpoint advance to the next and exhaustion produces a generic error. Missing transaction/receipt/block/log responses advance to a backup rather than prematurely settling an action as missing. The worker reads MANDATE_RECEIPT_RPC_FALLBACK_URLS; the primary chain config and chain confirmation gates remain unchanged. Tests cover actual HTTP RPC failover, wrong-chain rejection, refusal of remote cleartext HTTP, duplicate endpoints, parser boundaries, and a real Anvil/Safe receipt recovered after the configured primary refuses connection. No transaction submission is retried by this adapter.

## Phase 52 — chain-verified exact-byte transaction broadcast failover

Changed branch/fields: the API broadcaster now accepts ordered per-chain backups from MANDATE_BROADCAST_RPC_FALLBACK_URLS. Before any endpoint receives raw bytes, it must answer eth_chainId with the requested chain ID. The broadcaster sends the original caller-signed raw transaction unchanged and accepts only the locally calculated hash. If a provider returns an ambiguous transport/JSON-RPC failure, the broadcaster first checks that endpoint for the same hash, then tries the next verified endpoint with the exact same signed bytes; already-known errors count as success only if that endpoint can find the exact transaction or receipt. Every configured endpoint is bounded to HTTPS/loopback HTTP, redirect rejection, a 5-second request timeout, a 64-KiB response limit, and four endpoints maximum (primary plus three backups). Wrong-chain endpoints never receive raw transaction bytes. Exhaustion remains RPC_UNAVAILABLE so the existing hash/idempotency lifecycle retains its established retry/reconciliation path; this does not generate or sign new transactions or alter nonce/fee fields.

## Frontend-to-Anvil account-verification regression — 2026-10-07

- Branch/field changed: `EvmAccountEnrollmentVerifier` now parses Ethereum JSON-RPC quantities (`eth_chainId`, `eth_blockNumber`) with canonical quantity syntax, separate from byte-string parsing. Odd-length valid quantities such as `0x9` are accepted; malformed/non-canonical values such as `0x09` return `RPC_UNAVAILABLE`. Added `packages/adapters/test/evm-account-enrollment-verifier.test.ts`.
- RED: `npm test -- --reporter=dot packages/adapters/test/evm-account-enrollment-verifier.test.ts` failed first with `ChainVerificationError: ACCOUNT_NOT_ENROLLED` at the old `parseHex` on `0x9`; the malformed-quantity expectation also failed because `0x09` was accepted.
- MODIFIED focused: same command → `Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 2 passed (2)`; exit 0. `npm run typecheck` → no diagnostics; exit 0. Full backend unit suite: `npm test -- --reporter=dot` → `Test Files 46 passed | 10 skipped (56); Tests 310 passed | 51 skipped (361)`; exit 0.
